Now I see the issue. Looking at the checkpoint loading code:

```python
if (logdir / "latest.pt").exists():
    checkpoint = torch.load(logdir / "latest.pt")
    agent.load_state_dict(checkpoint["agent_state_dict"])
    tools.recursively_load_optim_state_dict(agent, checkpoint["optims_state_dict"])
    agent._should_pretrain._once = False
```

The checkpoint loads agent state and optimizer state, but does NOT restore the step counter (`logger.step`). The step counter is set during prefilling:


The prefill phase increments the logger step, and the agent's internal step counter is derived from it. When a checkpoint is restored, it only recovers the model weights and optimizer state, not the step counters themselves. So the logger step starts fresh from wherever it was initialized, which means the training loop's step tracking gets out of sync with what the checkpoint actually represents. The prefill phase generates new random episodes since only the model weights are restored, not the replay buffer data. To properly continue training from step 112K, I'd need to also copy the `train_eps/` and `eval_eps/` directories containing the replay buffer, though these can be quite large.

The step counter won't reflect the original 112K either—the checkpoint doesn't save logger.step, so prefill resets it to 5000, meaning training will run from step 5000 to 300K instead of continuing from 112K. The model weights are restored though, so performance should roughly pick up where it left off despite the step counter mismatch.

But this creates a real problem: the logs won't show "从 step 112K 恢复" as expected. I need to figure out how to preserve the step count across checkpoints. Looking at the agent initialization, `agent._step` is derived from `logger.step` divided by action_repeat, so I need to either save and restore the step counter directly or find another way to inject the correct step value. will run from effective step ~5000 to 300000, meaning the model gets an additional 295K steps of training on top of its existing 112K steps. The real issue is that without restoring the step counter or adjusting the target steps, I'd end up with ~407K total steps instead of the intended 300K. I should also consider whether the prefill strategy will work without the original replay buffer, since DreamerV3 relies heavily on that data.

Looking at the configuration, the training episodes are stored in `logdir/train_eps/`, which the code sets up automatically if no explicit traindir is specified. The most practical fix would be to stop the current training run, copy over both the `train_eps` and `eval_eps` directories from the original checkpoint, and then resolve the step counter issue before restarting.

I'm tracing through the prefill logic now — if we copy the training episodes, the `count_steps` function would detect that we already have more steps than the prefill threshold, so it would skip the prefill phase entirely and resume from where we left off based on the existing episode count. So copying train_eps is actually essential for proper resumption—without it, the replay buffer starts fresh even though the model weights are from a much later checkpoint, which will tank performance. I need to kill the current training run that started from step 5000 with an empty buffer, copy over both train_eps and eval_eps from the original experiment, and restart from there.

'\\n                    f'CIVO pattern detected!',\\n                    flush=True,\\n                )\\n        self.prev_delta_ve = self.delta_ve\\n        self.prev_ve_ratio = self.ve_ratio\\n        return self.alert_level\\n\\n\\n\\nclass Dreamer(nn.Module):\\n    def __init__(self, obs_space, act_space, config, logger, dataset):\\n        super(Dreamer, self).__init__()\\n self._should_train = tools.Every(batch_steps / config.train_ratio)\\n        self._should_pretrain = tools.Once()\\n        self._should_reset = tools.Every(config.reset_every)\\n        self._should_expl = tools.Until(int(config.expl_until / config.action_repeat))\\n        self._metrics = {}\\n        # this is update step\\n        self._step = logger.step // models.ImagBehavior(config, self._wm)\\n        if (\\n            config.compile and os.name != \\\"nt\\\"\\n        ):  # compilation is not supported on windows\\n            self._wm = torch.compile(self._wm)\\n            self._task_behavior = torch.compile(self._task_behavior)\\n        reward = lambda f, s, a: self._wm.heads[\\\"reward\\\"](f).mean()\\n        self._expl_behavior = dict(\\n            greedy=lambda: self._task_behavior,\\n            random=lambda: expl.Random(config, act_space),\\n            plan2explore=lambda: expl.Plan2Explore(config, self._wm, []\\n                if self._config.video_pred_log:\\n                    openl = self._wm.video_pred(next(self._dataset))\\n                    self._logger.video(\\\"train_openl\\\", ```python
def count_steps(folder):
    return sum(int(str(n).split("-")[-1][:-4]) - 1 for n in folder.glob("*.npz"))
```

Line 287-289:
```python
step = count_steps(config.traindir)  # counts steps from train_eps/*.npz files
logger = tools.Logger(logdir, config.action_repeat * step)  # logger.step = action_repeat * step
```

So with the copied train_eps directory:
- `count_steps` will count all the .npz files and compute the total steps


- `logger.step` gets initialized to `action_repeat * step`, which means if the original had around 56K environment steps, that translates to roughly 112K action-repeat steps for the logger
- Since I already have the train_eps from the original run, prefill will be zero and the checkpoint will load the model weights from that 112K step mark, allowing training to properly resume from where it left off
